## E-222 / M-221 regimen: uncapped solver, mid-size check of the counter model (Claude)

### What was done
- User said "onwards yes" to looking into a solver without the field cap. The cap was not in the algorithm: it was range checks on `p` and `n` and an O(p) brute-force point count in `solve-prime-file`. Added an optional `group_order` to the input (supplied by the generator, which computes it by baby-step giant-step): counted and cross-checked below 4e7, above that the Hasse bound and divisibility are checked; `p` and `n` now up to 2^50 (trial-division primality). Tests: a field above the old cap (p = 67108859) recovers the planted log and the matched rho verifies; a missing or Hasse-violating order and a disagreeing order are rejected. 13 tests pass.
- Mid-size panel (`evidence/run_mid_panel.py`): 40 runs on 12 instances, subgroup orders of 26 to 31 bits, F = 128 and 512, 8 matched-rho runs per instance. All recovered the planted log; all rho runs verified; rho cheaper at every weight of the band in 40/40. Model against the runs: trials observed / predicted 1.001 (default, 24 runs) and 0.962 (H1, 16 runs), deterministic counters exact; elimination over-predicted by 16 to 18%. Distances at these sizes are 340 to 55,000 (quadrupling F cuts them by about 4; a 16-fold increase in n raises them by about 4).
- Cross-check: scaling the measured mid-size ratios (H1, F = 512) by sqrt(n_native / n) x (F / 2^20) x (bits ratio) gives log2 distance 97.8 (E-222) and 96.6 (M-221), against the model's projection range 96.7 to 97.8 and 95.7 to 96.7.
- Added claim C7, the 40 outputs with a hash manifest (`evidence/mid/`), recompute and replay extensions (replay: nonce `1a2ab8ded8a84359`, 127 runs identical, binary blake3 `1368eaf1...`; it also still reproduces the band rows recorded under the earlier binary). Gate tool still refuses the draft only for the two producer-run `independent` flags and the artifact-store robustness rows.

### Decisions made
- Kept the O(p) count and the cross-check for small fields so existing behaviour is unchanged; the supplied order is trusted only above 4e7 and only for the Hasse bound and divisibility.
- Stated the extrapolation gap as it now is: about 190 bits beyond the largest measured subgroup (31 bits).

### Open threads
- No measured point beyond 31 bits; the per-trial cost at larger moduli needs the multi-word path (not exercised by `solve-prime-file`, which is word-sized); independent replay / validator with keyring signatures; robustness rows into the repository; pcc claims; EKS.

### Errors
- None that reached a commit.

## 05:05Z — Class-group isogeny walk into the JV weak class (kernel-polynomial odd isogenies)

- Implemented the full set of rational odd ℓ-isogenies (ℓ ∈ {3,5,7}) from a full-2-torsion curve over F_{p⁶}, the construction step named at the end of `research/jv_weak_coverage_20261111`. New in `src/cryptanalysis/jv_isogeny_walk.rs`:
  - `kernel_polys`: factor ψ_ℓ over F_{q³} (BigUint Frobenius powmod, Cantor–Zassenhaus), keep degree-(ℓ−1)/2 factors whose roots are a cyclic ℓ-subgroup by the mult-by-2 doubling-closure test (necessary and sufficient for prime ℓ, done in F_{q³}[x]).
  - `velu_image_through_h` / `map_odd_h`: Vélu through the kernel polynomial by symmetric functions (no individual roots), self-certified (A(x)/h² with deg A = ℓ, gcd(A,h)=1).
  - `walk_to_weak_classgroup`: BFS to a weak curve using these isogenies.
- Correctness gates `jv_isogeny_walk::tests::c1..c5`, all pass (42 s): C1 symmetric Vélu == per-root Vélu on split kernels (image + point map, ≥200 cases); C2/C4 certified isogenies are real-point homomorphisms incl. non-rational kernels; C3 doubling-closure is the decisive filter; C5 valid subgroup counts {0,1,2,ℓ+1} and completeness.
- `examples/jv_classgroup_coverage.rs` mirrors `jv_weak_coverage` with the class-group walk.
- Protocol `research/jv_classgroup_walk_20261112/PROTOCOL.md` frozen before runs; amendments 1 (walk-limit) and 2 (paired large-prime cap) disclosed.

- The doubling-closure test (X₂ permutes the roots of h) is the kernel-polynomial criterion; the Vélu degree/gcd self-cert is a secondary guard.
- Large primes compared paired (same curves, both walks, cap 3,000) because the class-group walk is far slower (ψ_ℓ factorization per curve); small primes keep cap 10,000 / 60 walks.

### Measured (unverified assertions)
- Weak-class reach, class-group vs rational-root (`jv_weak_coverage`):
  - p = 37: 41/60 = 68% vs 24/60 = 40% (non-overlapping Wilson intervals); 93% of found walks use a new odd step.
  - p = 53: 10/14 = 71% (partial, cut by the 3,000 s wall) vs 25/60 = 42%; 90% use a new odd step.
- The improvement is caused by the new isogenies (odd-step usage), confirming the mechanism.

- Large-prime numbers (p ≥ 1009, paired cap 3,000) are being gathered; both walks reach the weak class rarely at a feasible cap (the weak locus is beyond a feasible 2,3,5,7-isogeny radius at scale).
- Only ℓ ∈ {3,5,7}; the quadratic-h branch of the family is out of scope. Not a cost result — no leaderboard row moves.

- First C3/C5 test assertions were wrong (assumed the simple 0/1/2 isogeny count; full rational ℓ-torsion gives ℓ+1 subgroups). Replaced with valid-subgroup-count and completeness invariants; the implementation was correct.
- The test build needed data files (`curve25519_f1_targetwalk`, `efd_coordinate_charts`) hydrated from the artifact store.
- The large-prime cap-10,000 class-group runs did not complete on the container (a failed walk factors ψ_ℓ at up to 10,000 curves); switched to the paired cap-3,000 design.
